MINISTRY OF PERSONNEL, P.G. & PENSIONS 
(Department of Personnel & Training) 

New Delhi, the 16th January, 1997 

S.O. 209.—In exercise of the powers conferred by Sec¬ 
tion 5 read with section 6 of the Delhi Special Police Estab¬ 
lishment Act, 1946 (Act No. 25 of 1946), the Central Gov¬ 
ernment with the consent of Government of Rajasthan vide 
Home (Gr-5) Department Order No. F/14(6)-Homc-5/96 


dated 28th February, 1996 hereby extends the powers and 
jurisdiction of the members of the Delhi Special Police Estab¬ 
lishment to the whole of the State of Rajasthan lor investi¬ 
gation of offences punishable under sections 307, 353 and 
188 of Indian Penal Code, sections 3, 4. 5 of Terrorist and 
Disruptive Activities (prevention) Act, 1987, sections >8, 20 
& 21 of Narcotic Drugs and Psychotropic Substances Act, 
1985 und Sections 12 read with 3 and 6 Passport Act, 1967 
. and abetments, conspiracies in relation to and in connection 
with the said offences and any offences committed in the 
course of the said transaction arising out of the same facts 
in regard to Case No. 2/95 dated 9th March, 1995 of Police 
Station Sum, District Jaisalmer, Rajasthan regarding illicit 
transaction of Narcotic drugs and the illicit possessions of 
arms. 

[No. 228/25/96-AVD. II] 
S. C. TEWAP.Y, Dy. Secy.

New Delhi, the 2nd January, 1997 
STAMPS 

S.O, 210.—In exercise of the powers conferred by 
clause (b) of sub-section (1) of section 9 of the Indian 
Stamp Act, 1899 (2 of 1899), the Central Government here¬ 
by permits M/s. Assam Company Limited, Calcutta to pay 
consolidated stamp duty of rupees sixteen lakhs fifty thou¬ 
sands only on 22,00,000 secured redeemable non-convertible 
debentures bearing distinctive numbers 001 to 22,00,000 of 
the face value of rupees one hundred each at par of the 
aggregate value of rupees twenty two crorcs only to be issued 
by the said company. 

[No. 1/97-ST—F. No. 15/1 J'96-ST]

(Department of hconomie Affairs) 

(Banking Division) 

New Delhi, the 9th January, 1997 

S.O. 211.—Whereas on 5th November, 196.9, a scheme of 
amalgamation of the Bank of Behar LUl., Patna, with the 
State. Bank of India, was sanctioned hy the Central Govern¬ 
ment in exercise of the powers conferred by and in accord¬ 
ance with Section 45 of the Banking Regulation Act, 1949; 

Whereas under suh-clause (ix) of clause 6 of the said 
Scheme, the State Bank of India was required to make a final 
valuation of the assets of the Bank of Behar Ltd,, which 
have been provisionally valued on the prescribed date, on 
the expiry of twelve years from the prescribed date ; 


Whereas the State Bank of India has represented that in 
view of large number of assets involved and the recovery 
of most of the items yet to be realised, in spite of its efforts, 
it has not been able to make the final valuation within the 
time specified in sub-clause (ix) of clause 6 of the Scheme 
of amalgamation ; 

And whereas the Central Government, after consultation 
with the Reserve Bank of India, is satisfied that a difficulty 
has arisen in giving effect to the scheme of amalgamation, 
which it is necessary to remove by extending the time, within 
which the final valuation of assets is required to be made ; 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred by 
clause 20 of the scheme of amalgamation dated 5th Novem¬ 
ber, 1969 of the Bank of Bebar Ltd., Patna, with the State 
Bank of India, the Central Government hereby directs that 
the State Bank of Tndia shall, in consultation with and with 
the approval of the Reserve Bank of India, value the assets 
of the Bank of Behar Ltd., Patna, which have not been 
realised and valued, within the period of twenty-eight years 
from the prescribed date. 

[No, 15/5 94-BOA] 

P, MOHAN, Uircctoi 

FffTOft, 9 TOTOff, 1997

section (2) of section 14 of the Indian Medicine Central

after consulting the Central Council of Indian Medicine,

second schedule to the said Act, namely :— 

In the said Part, under the heading “Himachal Pradesh’' 
against serial number 24C relating to Himachal 
Pradesh University, Simla, in column 4, for the 
entry “From 1986 to 1992”, the entry “From 1986 
onwards” shall be substituted.

New Delhi the 14th. laiiuary, 1997 

S.O. 215.—Whereas in pursuance of clause (b) of sub¬ 
section (J) of section 3 of the Indian Medical Council of 
India Act, 1956 (102 of 1956), Dr. S. R. Maralihalli, Karna¬ 
taka has been elected on 20th September, 1996 by the Senate 
of Kuvempu University to be a member of the Medical 
Council of India from 20th September, 1996; 

Now, therefore, in pursuance of sub-section ll) of sec¬ 
tion 3 of the said Act, the Central Government hereby makes 
the following further amendment in the notification of the 
Government of India in the then Ministry of Health number 
S.O. 138. dated the 9th January. I960, namely ;— 

in the said notification, under the heading “Elected

MINISTRY OF COAL 
New Delhi, the 21st January, 1997 ■ 

S.O. 222,- — Whereas by the notification of the Government of India in the Ministry of Ccal No. S.Q. 12 1 5 
dated 27th March, 1996 issued under sub-section (1) of section 7 of the Coal Bearing Areas (Acquisition and 
Development) Act, 1957 (20 of 1957) and published in Part II, Section 3, Sub-Section (ii) of the Gazette of India 
dated the 20th April, 1996, the Central Government gave notice of its intention to acquire lands and rights in the 
locality specified in the Schedule appended to that notification; 

And whereas the competent authority, in pursuance of section 8 of the said Act has made his report to the 
Central Government; 

And whereas the Cential Government after considering the said report and after consulting the Govern¬ 
ment of Orissa is satisfied ihat the lands measuring2628.0S acres (approximately) or 1053.56 hectares (approxi¬ 
mately) described in the Schedule appended hereto should be acquired; 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred by sub-section (1) of section 9 of the Coal Bearing Area 
(Acquisition and Development) Act, 1957 (20 of 1957), the Cential Government hereby declares that the land' 
measuiing 2628.05 acres (approximately) or 1063.56 hectares (approximately) dcsciibed in the said SchcduL 
are hereby acquired. 

The Plan No. MCL/SAMB/CGM/(CP&P)/96/6 dated the 12th July, 1996 of the area covered by this notifi. 
Ctttion may be inspected in the oflice ol the Collector, Angul (Oiissa) or in the oflice of the Coal Conti oiler, 
!, Council House Street, Calcutta or in the office of the Mahanadi Coalfields Limited (Coiporate Planning 
Department), Anand Vihar, Post Office U.C.E., Burla, District Sambalpur (Orissa).

MINISTRY. OF PETRCLEUM & NATURAL

New Delhi, the 17th January, 1997 

S.O. 223.—Whereas it appears to the Central 
Government that it is necessary in the public 
ipWtst that for the transport of Petroleum and 
hd ural G ,s from Kanawada E.P.S. to G.E. Apar 
in Gujaraf State pipeline should be laid by the 
Gas Authority of India Ltd. 

And whereas it appears that for the purpose of 
laying such pipeline, it is necessary to acquire the 
right; of us :r in the land described in the schedule 
annexed hereto ; 


Now, therefore, in exercise of the powers con- 
fa rred by sub-section (1) of the Section 3 of the 
Petroleum and Minerals Pipelines (Acquisition of 
Right of User in the Land) Act 1962 (50 of 1962), 
the Central Government hereby declares its inten¬ 
tion to acquire the right of user therein; 

Provided that any person interested in the said 
End may within 21 days from the date of this 
notification, object to the laying of the pipeline 
under the land to the Competent Authority, Gas 
Authority of India Ltd. Darpart Building, R. C. 
Djtt Road, Vadodara. 

And every person making such an objection 
shall also state specifically whether he wishes to 
be hoard in person or by legal practitioner.

Government that it is necessary in the public

Gas Authority of India Ltd.

laying such pipeline, it is necessary to acquire the 
right of user in the land described in the schedule 
annexed hereto ;

the Central Government hereby declares its inten¬

under the land to the Competent Authority, Gas 
Authority of India Ltd. Darpan. Building, R. C.

shall also state specifically whether he wishes to

New Delhi, the 20th January, 1997 

S.O. 226.—Whereas it appears to the Central 
Government that it is necessary in the public 
interest that for the transport of Petroleum and 
Natural Gas from ACF Thai to Usar Gas P'pe- 
line in Maharashtra State pipeline should be laid 
by the Gas Authority of India Ltd. 

And whereas it appears that for the purpose of 
laving such pipeline, it is necessary to acqirre the 
right of user in the land described in the schedule 
annexed hereto ; 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the powers con¬ 
ferred bv sub-section (1) of the Section 3 of the 
Petroleum and Minerals Pipelines (Acquisition of 


Right of User in the Land) Act, 1962 (50 of 1962), 
the Central Government hereby declares its inten¬ 
tion to acquire the right of user therein; 

Provided that any person mterested in the said 
land may, within 21 days from the date of this 
notification, object to the laying of the pipeline 
under the land to the Competent Authority, Gas 
Author’ty of India Ltd., Alibag (Maharashtra). 

And every person making such an objection 
shall also state specifically whether he wishes to 
be heard in person or by legal practitioner.

1, 1997/RW 12, 1918 


New Delhi, the 20th January, 1997 


S.0.228.— Whereas by the notification of the Government of India in 
'the Ministry of Petroleum and Natural Gas No. S,0, and date as 
mentioned in the Schedule below issued under sub-section (11 of 
section 6 of the petroleum and Minerals Pipelines (Acquisition of 
Right of User in Land’) Act, 1962 (50 of 1962) the Central 
Government acquired the right of user in the lands specified in 
the Schedule appended to that notification; 


And whereas in the exercise of the powers conferred bv sub¬ 
section (4) of section 6 of the said Act, the Central Government 
vested the right of user in the said lands, free from all 
encumbrances, in the Indian Oil Corporation Limited; 


And whereas the Competent /^thoritv has made a report to the 
Cen ral Government that the pipeline for the purpose of transport 
of Petroleum from Kandla in the State of Gu.larat to Bhatinda in 
the State of Fun,lab has been laid and the said lands have been 
restored to the land owners and obtained no claim certificate 
from them, So the operation may be terminated in respect of the 
lands the aescriPtion of which in brief is specified in the 
Schedule annexed to this notification; 


Now. therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred bv rule 
4 of the Petroleum Pipelines ( Acquisition of Right of User in 
Land) Rules, 1963, the Central Government hereby declares the 
dates mentioned in column 6 of‘they said schedule as the date of 
termination of operation.

and no d'spute ex : st between the parties. He also stated 
that no dispute award may be given in this case. Tn view 
of the statement of the workman no dispute exists between 
the parties and a no dispute award is, therefore, given in 
this ca' e leaving the parties to bear their own costs.

APPEARANCES: 

Shri A. K. Gupta—for the Union. 

Shri Jagat Aioia with Mrs, K. Bajaj—for the Manage¬ 
ment. 

AWARD 

The Central Government in the Ministry of Labour, vide 
its Order No. L-l20l2/169/89-D-2(A), dated nil has refined 
the following industrial dispute to this Tribunal for adjudica¬ 
tion : 

“Whether the action of the Management of Punjab and 
Sindh Bank in shifting the date of annual increment 
of Shri B, K, Khandclwal from 1-4-88 to 1-5-88 
and deducting Rs. 158.54 from his Savings Bank 
Account is justified 7 If not, to what relief is the 
workman entitled ?” 

2. Shri B. K. Khandclwal, the workman concerned, was 
posted as Clerk-cum-Cashier- at the Asaf Ali Road Branch 
of the Punjab and Sindh Bank, New Delhi. 

3. It is stated by the Union that treating the workman 
concerned unauthonsedly absent from his duty on 21-8-87, 
15-3-88 and 21-4-88 and deducting wages for these days from 
his salary is arbitrary, mala fide, unlawful and ayainst the 
provisions of the Bipartite Settlement /Awards governing ser¬ 
vice conditions of the Bank employees. The plea of the 
Union justifying absence, from .duty of the workman on these 
days is that on 21-8-87 tho entire branch staff, including the 



934 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : FEBRUARY 1,1997, MAGHA 12, 1918 [Part II— Sec. 3(u)] 


workman, was not allowed to join duty. On 15-3-88, there 
was a Bharat Band anu on 21-4-88 at the time ot surprise 
checking at the branch, the workman concrened had left the 
branch, lor a while, it is further aliened by the Union that 
the management was not competent to debit the sum of 
Rs. 158.M trom the Savings Bunk Account of the woikman 
concerned as the workman had not authorised the manage¬ 
ment to do so. 

4. In their written statement, the management have denied 
the aoove allegations, n has been stated oy the management 
that the workman concerned was absent on 21-8-«7, ijj-J-08 
and 21-4-88. Lor these three days oi unauthorised absence, 
Ins annual inciemcm, lading on 30-4-88 was postponed to 
3-5-88 by three days, irnuer due intimation to him. it is 
further mated that toe deduction of Rs. 158.54 was made 
as the same had been paid wrongiy to the woikman con¬ 
cerned, because ot the postponement @1 the date of his annual 
increment. Since the said amount had been wrongiy credited 
in his Savings Bang Account, the same was adjusted by 
debuting his Savings Bank Account, it is further stated that 
the workman concerned did not attend the duties on 21-8-87 
and 15-3-88. A surprise check was earned out on 21-4-88 
by the staff of the rtegional Office, during which the work¬ 
man was not found present in the branch and the wages for 
the day, were deducted treating him absent unauthorisediy. 

5. The Union has filed 8 documents vide its list of docu¬ 
ments dated 5-8-91. No oral evidence has been led by the 
Union. 

6. The management have filed three documents vide list 
dated 15-8-91 and have examined Sbri Amrit Lai Sethi, 
Manager as MW.l. 

7. I have heard the representatives of both the parties 
and have gone through the evidence on record. 

8. Undisputably, the woikman concerned did not attend the 
duties on 21-8-87 and 15-3-88. Undisputably also on 21-4-88, 
when the surprise check of the branch was conducted by 
the staff of the Regional Office, the workman concerned 
wa» not found present .n the branch. There is no evidence 
on record, whereby it could bo proved that the workman 
concerned was present in the branch or had left the branch 
with due permiss.on of the branch manager or any superior. 
There is also no evidence on record, whereby it cou.d be 
proved that the workman concerned absented himself from 
duty on 21-8-87 and 15-3-88 on sanctioned leave. The man¬ 
agement, therefore, is fully justified treating these three clays 
as unauthorised absence from duty and deducting wages for 
the same, as a consequence of which, date of his annual incre¬ 
ment, falling on 30-4-88 was postponed to 3rd May, 1988 
by three days. I do not find anything wrong in it. 

9. Hence, held that the action of the management in shift- 
in" the date of annual increment of the workman concerned 
from 1-4-88 to 1-5-88 is fully justified. It is further held 
that the workman concerned was not entitled to be paid wages 
for the days he remained unauthorisediy absent, as aforesaid 
and consequently was not entitled to the sum pf Rs, 158.54 
due to postponement of the dote o'f his annual increment. 
Hence the management is also fully justified in debiting 
Rs. 158.54 from the Savings Bank Account of the workman 
concerned, wherein, the said amount had been wrongly cre¬ 
dited. Consequently, the workman concerned is not entitled 
to any relief. 

27th December, 1996. 

GANPATI SHARMA, Presiding Officer

1. Central Government, Ministry of Labour, New Delhi, 
vide its Notification No. L-12012/732/88 D.il (A) dated 
20,4 (year not given) has referred the following dispute for 
adjudication to tiffs Tribunal—• 

Whether the action of the management of Punjab 
National Bank in terminating die services of Shri 
Sobhnath Sub-staff is justified 7 If not (o what 
relief is the workman entitled ? 

2. The concerned workman Sobhnath in his claim state¬ 
ment has alleged that ho was engaged as peon from 1-1-80 
to 13-2-86 in broken periods at Varanasi Branch of the 
former Hindustan Commercial Bank Limited as peon. In 
para 4 of the claim statement the details of number of work¬ 
ing days have been given which would go to show that from 
1-1-85 upto 31-12-85, he had worked for 348 days and from 
January 86 to 13-2-86 he had worked for 44 days. It is 
alleged tha', since he had completed 240 days in n year 
preceding the date of termination and since no retrenchment 
compensation and notice pay was given the termination is 
bad. Beside there has been breach of Sections 25-G and H 
of I. D. Act. 

3. Now the opposite party Punjab National Bank who has 
{topped into the shoes of Hindustan Commercial Bank, they 
are liable to reinstate the concerned workman. 

4. I he opposite party bank has alleged that concerned 
workman is not their employee. In any case he was appoin¬ 
ted as temporary employee, he has got no right at all. It 
has not been specifically denied as to whether number of 
days os given in para 4 of claim statement are correct or 
not. 

5. In the rejoinder nothing new has been said. 

6. In support of his claim the concerned workman lias 
examined himself as Sobhnath WW-1 whereas opposite party 



[wpr II—sfc a (ii) ] 


strictrTrim:rpvrff i, 1997/mR 12 , 191 s 


935 


has examined A. K. Tayal MVV-1 their Assistant Manager. 
Besides the concerned workman has filed Ext. W-l *o W-12. 
Some of these papers i elates to proceedings before Al.C(C), 
some of which in the nature of telegram and letter issued 
by the management to discontinue the services of tlie con¬ 
cerned workman as temporary hand. 

7. At the outset it may be mentioned that the concerned 
workman Subbnn’h It is not adduced any evidence regarding 
bieach of Sections 25-F and H of I, D. Act. Hence ths 
issue is decided against him for want of proof. 

8. As regards the claim of the concerned workman for 
having completed 2 AO days in a year preceding the date of 
termination, it may be mentioned tint i 1 was spec.lically 
pleaded in para 4 of the claim statement that he hud comple¬ 
ted 348 days from 1-1-85 to 31-12-85 and for 41 days from 
1-1-86 to 13-2-86. This fact has not bran specifically denied 
in the written statement. Hence, it should be taken to be 
correct. Besides Sobhnath WYV-1 has specific.div staled that 
ho had worked from 1-1-86 to 13-10-86 as alleged in rhe 
written statement. In the cross examination thift fact has 
not been challenged. Only it was inquired as to whether 
letter was issued to him nr not and (h’t he was riven tem¬ 
porary employment. A, K. Tayal MW-1 has denied that 
concerned wnikman has worked between 1-1-86 to 13-6 86. 
Ho has further stated that there are no document available. 
I am not inclined to believe his evidence. He is an officer 
of the bank he is not expected to remember ten yen's old 
facts. If he has spoken about this factor he must have done 
so after going through the records. Hence his evidence that 
papers are not available is not correct.' it appears to me 
that these papers have been withheld from the court for 
oblique purpose. Hence I am inclined to draw adverse 
inference against them. Thus on the ground (hat concerned 
workman’s version has not been specially denied in the 
written statement and because the evidence of concerned work¬ 
man is uncross examined and further as Jncnmen’s have 
been withheld I believe the version of the concerned work¬ 
man. It is according 1 v held th-'t the concerned v/rnkman 
has completed more than 240 days preceding the date of 
retrenchment. Admittedly bo retrenchment compensation and 
notice pay was given to him. Hence Ibis term'wiFon is bed 
in law being in breach of Section 25-F of 1. D. Act. 

9. In view of above discussion my award is that termina¬ 
tion of services of the concerned workman is bad in law and 
he is entitled for reinstatement from the dale of reference.

AWARD 

1. Central Government, Ministry of Labour, New Delhi, 
>;iua its NcHificatir.n No, L-12017./730/87-D.II (A) dated 
13-7-88 has referred the following dispute tor adjudication 
to this Tribunal— 

Kya Bank of Baroda he prabandhtanirt ki Sri Mangal 
Singh ko 16-3-86 sc driver bhatta punch shooru na 
karne ki tatha es sthan par jaha use dand diya gaya 
tha. ten at na karne ki karwai nyayochit hai ? Yadt 
nahi to karmkar kis anutosh ka haqdar hai 7 

2. Following facts are undisputed : 

4 he concerned workman Mangal Singli was engaged as 
reon. Later on by order dated H-4-37 and 18-7-77 
he was asked to petform the duties of driver as 
well for which driver allowance was being paid to 
him. It appears that subsequently he was charge- 
sheeted for certain misconduct and by order dated 
16-3-81 he was awarded punishment by way of 
stoppage of four increments in addif on to his 
withdrawal of special allowance of the driver for a 
period of five years, The period of this punish¬ 
ment expired on 15-5-86. 

3. The grievance of the concerned workman is that inspite 
of passage of this time he has not been res'ored special 
allowance of driver. It has been further alleged that by way 
of harrassment he was transferred to Nawabganj Branch of 
Bare Ily to perform the duties of peon cum driver. Since 
it was a case of victimization the concerned workman showed 
his reluctancp to go there. 

4. The opposite party has filed written statement in which 
it is alleged that as concerned workman had refused to go 
to _ Nawabganj Branch as peon-cum-driver he is not being 
paid driver's allowance. Further driver allowance is subject 
to performance of duty of a driver which he had failed to 
do. 

5. In the rejo nder nothing new has been said. 

6. From the above it will be seen that the concerned work¬ 
man has been transferred after the passage of period of 
punishment but he has not joined there under the pretext 
that it was a case of victimization as others were retained 
at their respective places. In my opinion, transfer [s an 
incident of serv ; ce, An employee is bound to ro on transfer 
as per direction of employer unless it is a result of malafide. 
In the claim statement it has been only alleged that this 
transfer was effected by way of victimization. In my opinion 
it is not enough. The particulars of victimization ought to 
have been given. It should have been clarified as to who 
was the person with whom the concerned workman was hud 
variance and on whose behest this transfer order was passed. 
In its absence, mere assertion that the transfer is because of 
V'Ctimizafion is rot enough, The concerned workman was 
given an opportunity on 17-7-96 to adduce his evidence but 
he F"lcd to avail it. Thus on facts too it is not es'ahllshed 
'hN fill's transfer was m?de out of malnfide inlmlion. Hence 
as transfer has not been effected due to malalide intwtion 
or by way of victimization and if the concerned workman 



936 


THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : FEBRUARY 1, 1997/MAGHA 12, 1918 [Part II— Sec. 3(ii)] 


had failed to join at Nawabganj Branch in pursuance of the 
order of transfer issued by the management my award is that 
the action of the management in not giving driver’s allowance 
is justified and the concerned vvotkman is not entitled for 
any relief. 

7- Reference is answered accordingly.

1. Central Government, Ministry of Labour, New Delhi, 
vide its Notification No. 12012/65/92-I.R, (D-II) dated 
7 8-92, has referred the following dispute for adjudientian 
to this Tribunal— 

Whether the action of the management of United Bank 
of India in not granting full scale wages of sub- 
staff to Shri Mooi Chand, Part-time Sweeper w.e.f. 
January, 1985, is justified ? If not, to what relief 
is the workman entitled ? 

2. The case of the concerned workman Mool Chand is 
that originally he was engaged on 1-5-76 at Maualma Branch 
of the opposite party United Bank of India as Part-time 
Sweeper and was being given 1 /3 of regular service by way 
of wages. Lateron the area of the Bank increased and he 
was being paid wages (E> 1/2 of the salary of the regular 
employee w.e.f. 1-9-78. Lateron area of Bank for sweeping 
was increased furiier. Hence he was being wages at full 
rate. This arrangement continued upto 1981. With effect 
from March, 1981 this arrangement was stopped and the 
area was reduced and he was paid 1 / 2 of the wages of the 
regular employee. It is alleged that he still sweeping the 
entire area the opposite partv bank has illegally reduced his 
wages by adopting unfair labour practice, 

3. The opposite party bank has filed reply in which it has 
been admitted that the concerned workman was employed 
as Part-time Sweeper w.e.f. 1-5-76 and he was being n>'d 
1/3 of the salary. It is abo adnrtted that w.e.f. 1 - 9-78 
the wages of concerned V'orkman were raised to half of the 
salary. It is specifically denied that any time wages were 
enhanced to full as Is being given to a regular employee. 
Hence the concerned workman is not entitled for full wages. 

4. In the rejoinder nothing new has been alleged. 

5. The one point which call for determination is as to 
whether the wages of concerned workman were raised to full 
at any time after 1-9-78. 

6. It b pertinent to point out that the claim pet-tion the 
date from which full wages were given has not been men¬ 
tioned. No doubt the concerned workman in his evidence 
has spoken about it but I am not inclined to believe him 
that half of the waves given to the applicant for sweenmc 
4000 sqr. ft. area. Ext. M-l and M-2 are the conies of lease 
Deed which go to show the carpet area tentated building not 
exceed 4000 sq. ft. Hence accession for raising the wages 
of the concerned workman to the hill d ; d not arise at all. 
In this way thee is no question of raising wages to (he full 
would arise, Even the concerned workman in bis cross 
examination has s f ated that had to sweep 4000 sq. ft. area. 
In view of this admission and also be cause of evidence of 
Anin Kumar Srivastnva MW-1 who was the manager of the 
bank nt that time I come to the e ,inclusion that as area 
was sweening was not increased. The Bank had not raised 
the wages of the concerned workman to the full at any 
time. 

7. Hence the Bank is not liable to n,w to the concerned 
workman full scale of wages of n Snh-Staff to the concerned 
workman and he is not entitled for any relief. I award 
accordingly.

AWARD 

The Ministry of Labour, Government of India, New Delhi 
vide Notification No. L-12012/5S/93-TR (B-II) dated 29-9-93 
referred a dispute between the Management of Allahabad 
Bank, Regional Office, Panbazar, Guwahnti and its workman 
Shri Shahjahan Ali represented by General Secretary North- 
Eastern Reg'on Allahabad Bank Employees Association, Fancy 
Bazar, Guv r.hati for adjudication with copies to the parties. 
On receipt of the notification a case was registered and notices 
were cent to the parties to appear and to file their written 
statement. Roth the parties appeared and filed their written 
statement, The issue reads as follows— 

“Whether the action of the Management of Allahabad 
Bank in dismissing the service of Md, Shahjahan 
Ali, ex-Cashier-m-charge. Tetenbari Branch of 
Allahabad Bank by an order dated 4-5-92 is justi¬ 
fied ? If not to what relief the workman is entitled 
to 7” 

The reference is against the order of dismissal of woifc- 
man Shahjahan AU from the service of the Bank of the 
ground of misconduct with effect from 4-5-92.The manage¬ 
ment brought as many as five charges against the workman 
alleging that bis acts of emission/commission tantamount to 
gross misconduct in terms of Clause 19,5(J) of the Bipartite 
Settlement 1966, The management instituted a domestic 
enquiry to enquire the charges. Tn the enquiry the workman 
has filed his written statement by denying the charges, both 
sides tendered evidence and the witness examined bv each 
side were discharged after cross examination. The ertqnirv 
officer found the workman guilty of misconduct on being 
satisfied that the charges levelled against him have been foui'-i 
proved. The disciplinary authority on careful consideration 
of the report, he'd a personal hearing nimbi, and came to the 
conclusion that the charges levelled against him have been 
found well established and finally dismissed him from service 
with effect from 4-5-92. Against the order of dismissal he 
prferred an appeal before the appellate authority hut get 
no relief. Then a dispute was raised before the Labour 
Commissioner. Guwahati. Tn the conciliation proceedin' 1 
before the A'-'f. Labour Commissioner fbe dispute remained 
unresolved, The. matter was referred to the Central Govern¬ 
ment who in return referred the dispute to this Tribunal. 

The workman denied the charges all along and contested 
(he dispute to have legal remedy; and to prove hit -ase he 


examined himself and a Witness before this Tribunal. The 
management examined as many as six witnesses in support of 
iheir cn'c. Both sides proved number of documents into 

: Cl vice, 

A preliminary issue was raised whether the domestic enquiry 
held was fair and proper as repaired by law. After hearing 
Ihe parlies and an perusal of the evidences laid down by either 
sido it v-iis held that the domestic enquiry was not fair and 
proper. Thereafter the management again tendered evidnees 
in addition to the evidences already tendered but the workman 
laid no further evidences. Both sides were assured that their 
evidences already tendered in connction with the preliminary 
issue shall be treated as evidence on merit of the rference. 
Both sids filed written argument which were explained by 
oral submission. 

The word gross misconduct is defined in Clause 19.5(1) 
of the Bipartite Settlement 1966 as follows— 

(I) Doing act prejudicial to the interest of the Bank. 

(11) Gross negligence. 

(ITU Negligence involving or likely to involve the Bank 
in serious loss, 

Section II-A of the Industrial Dispute Act empowers the 
Tribunal to satisfy itself whether misconduct is proved and 
to given any relief to Ihe workman including the award of 
any lesser punishment in lieu of discharge or dismissal. The 
Tribunal cannot interfere with the findings of 'fact recorded 
by the enquiry officer by making a fresh assessment of the 
value tn be attached to the evidence of the witness; but it 
can interfere if there is want of good faith, if thore is 
victimisation or unfair labour practice, if the management has 
been guilty of basic error nr violation of principle of natural 
justice and if on the basis of materials, the finding is comple¬ 
tely perverse. 

Exl. 2 is the chnrgesheet issued against the workman. 
Charge No. I is in respect of issue of cheque Book bearing 
leaves No. 017801—0178t0 to Shri S. C. Deb, an account 
bolder of the Bank at Sibsagar Branch, under his own band 
writing in the chcquc-book-rcgister without getting the same 
initialled/confirmed b’' the supervisory official, and thereby 
violated the norms of the Bank. He denied this charge but 
on enquiry the enquiry officer found the charge proved. 

Entitlement of cheque book is a right of every account 
holder. If. has been submitted by the learned counsel for 
the management that certain procedure is to be followed 
before nnv cheque book is issued to any account bolder ns 
per Bipartite Settlement fExt. 18). Herein this case Shri 
S. C. Deb submitted an application Ext. 10(1) (M-4) claiming 
cheque book. Though this was addressed to the manager it 
did not carry (lie signature of Manager or of Shri Shahjahan 
Ali. It is crossed by two strokes. Shahjahan Ali in his 
evidence admitted that be crossed it. Wit ess No, 6 was the 
Branch Manager of Ribsagar Branch at that time. He 
deposed that Shri Shabiaban Ali did not produce the reuuisi- 
tionship before him; and no authentication was obtained from

particulars shall be entered into the cheque issue register.

was not followed by (be Branch. Shahjahan Ali admitted 
tlint he made the entry into the cheque issuing register. Ext. 
10 contains all the documents marked as M-1 to M-29 is the 
cheque issuing register. As many ns 62 numbers of account

cheque book, Not a single entry benrs the signature of the

manager explained that he did not put his signature in the

be is doing over work regularly. He beinic a manager he

In support of this case management relied on. lybJujLLJ 
78 and LLJ March 1991 page 291. in my earlier order 
I hold the Domestic Enquiry unfair and improper. It is 
now settled law that if it is shown that tho employer lias held 
a pioper enquiry the Tnbunul would not be entitled to con¬ 
sider the propriety or correctness ot tne conclusion reached 
by the Enquiry Officer, but the Tribunal is entitled to consi¬ 
der the said eoneius.on if they appear to be perverse, or 
unfair and the final order passed against the workman amount¬ 
ed to v.ctimisaiion ot unfair iabour practice, The enquiry 
officer did not examine Shri 5. C, Deb, Shri Chandon Biswas,

management have 'failed to prove the ciiaigss against the 
workman. In tho light of tho above I find the decision 
in i963UJ)LLJ 78 not acceptable herein this case. 

It has been submitted on behalf of the management that 
the Bank hfls lost faith on the workman. It is true that trust 
and confidence arc the basis of the Banking instilutions. Once 
there i‘ loss of trust and confidence upon a particular emp¬ 
loyee, seivice of such employee for tho interest of the Bank 
matters most. The question of trust and confidence have

I AT 291. Number oi Case laws were discussed in this deci¬ 
sion. In llie above decision the Hon’bie Supreme Court ob¬ 
served iliac the Tribumd lias the discretion to award compen- 
saLon i aster cl of reinstatement if the circumstances are 
unusual or exceptional so ns lo make reinstatement in expe¬ 
dient or improper. Where the employer losses confidence on its 
employee who is discharging an office of tmst and confidence; 
reimbursement is -not desirable or expedient. Definitely it is 
my humble duty to see that the public intorsi on the working 
of the Bank is not affected by the Award. Even though the 
management found him guilty of misconduct neither the 
enquiry officer in his enquiry report nor the disciplinary 
authority in the final order of dismissal, mentions that the 
workman losses confidence of the authority and he can not 
be retained in the setv.cn of the Tank. Loss of confidence 
or faith must be unequivocally mentioned in the enquiry re¬ 
port as well as in the final order of dismissal but it is not 


mentioned. So I find the decision cited not acceptable and 
the submission of compensation in lieu of reinstatement 
favours no consideration. 

What I find is that the oider oi dismissal of the workman 
fiom the service of the Bank was based on. unfair labour 
practice and lack of good faith. As a result the order of 
dismissal has been found 1 not legally tenable and is hereby 
set aside, but the way be involved in the issues so promptly 
i”, ib>.’ d‘sc barge of his official duties is not above suspicion.

duties. The management is hereby directed to icinslalc him

This dispute coming on for final hearing before me in 
the presence of the workman in person and Sri D, V. Siibba 
Rat), advocate for management, upon hearing the arguments 
of both sides the court passed the following: 

AWARD 

(1) In this case reference is made by the Government of 
Biffin under Section 10(l)(d) of the ID. Act in the following 
terms : 



945 


[Utir-TI—*! 3(ii)l 


“Whether the action of management of Dock l abour 
Board, Visakhapatnam in terminating the services 
of Smt. G. Venkata Rathanam, Ex-female Nursing 
Orderly is justified ? If not, to '.'but relief 
the workman concerned is entitled ?" 

(2) Claim statement is filed by the workman stating that 
she resigned afler serving about 13 years with the respondent 
as a Female Nursing Grdeily belonging to Schedule Caste 
community and permanent employee. She stales that she 
was mentally up-set due to domestic affairs and she made 
several appeals to reconsider her cafe for reinstatement. I he 
respondent authorities first appointed her as Gardener and 
later as F.N.O. in the place of Smt. D. Nirmaja, who went 
on maternity lerv; from March. 1993 .and joined in June 
and after she joined, the petit oner was sent back as Gardener 
to Enginceting Section site office. She states that she has 
not taken her settlement dues so for and in the meanwhile 
the D.L.B. authorities wanted to coircct market rent on her 
quarter staling that she sublet the same which is false. She 
states that she is not mentally well due to domestic affairs 
and some other DLB workers who resigned and took their 
settlement dues, were reinstated into service and she may 
be considered for reinstatement as F.N.O. 

(3) Counter ; s filed by the respondent denying that the 
petitioner is a permanent employee in (he Dock Labour 
Board. It is stated that she esubmitted Iter resignation as 
Female Nursing Orderly on 12-2-85 and her resignation was 
accepted on 22-4-85. She requested for settlement of her 
P.F., gratuity by writing letter on 7-6-85. After her resigna¬ 
tion is accepted she cannot claim reinstatement stating that 
fhe was mentally up-set. It is further stated that the vacancy 
created by her resignation is filled and on sympathetic con¬ 
siderations. the petitioner was appointed as casual Gardener 
with the respondent and alro as Female Nursing Orderly on 
adhoc basis from 1-4-93 to 31-5-93 when the incumbent ap¬ 
plied for maternity leave and on her joining, the petitioner 
was again sent as casual Gardener on daily wage basis. 
It is stated that she sublet the quarter allotted to her and 
therefore she was evicted. It is denied that the others who 
resigned are reinstated and it is stated that the vacancy 
created by the petitioner's resignation was notified to the 
employment exchange and selection was conducted, in which, 
a wife of a senior driver who died of cancer was selected 
and appointed to the post and she also belongs to Scheduled 
Caste. Thus, it is pleaded that ns the petitioner resigned and 
the same was accepted as long back as in 1985, the petition 
is not maintainable and the same may be dismissed. 

(4) On behalf of the workman, she got herself examined 
as WW1 and no documents arc marked. On behalf of the 
management, the chief mrdical officer is examined as MW! 
and Rxs. Ml to M5 are marked. 

(5) Heard arguments of both sides. 

(6) The pomts that arise for consideration are : 

(1) Whether the petitioner's sendees are terminated ille¬ 

gally 7 

(2) To what relief is the petitioner entitled ? 

(7) Point No. 1.—Fhe petitioner deposes as WW1 talit she 
worked as Female Nursing Orderly for 13 years in the Dock 
Labour Board, Hospital, Visakhapatnam and she resigned to 
the said post on 12-2-85 and from 1985 March, she did not 
attend duty. Ex. M2 is the resignation letter dated 12-2-85 
admitted by her in her cross-examination, She deposes that 
one Kayva Mohana Rao, who is the General Secretary of 
SC and ST community, submitted an application to the man¬ 
agement in the month of April or May, 1985 withdrawing 
the resignation letter, on her behalf. She admits that Fx. 
Ml is the said letter withdrawing the resignation. Fx, Ml 
is dated 39-4-85 but MW1 denoses that her resignation was 
accepted bv the Deputy Chairman on 22-4-85 itself and re¬ 
lieving orders also were issued on 23-4-85 b.T-ing on the 
acceptance of the resignation. He further deposes that the 
relieving orders are sent to the petitioner by registered post 
but the same was returned with an endorsement that the 
addressee refused. Ex. M4 is the returned letter showing 
this endorsement dated 2-5-85, MW1 further states that 
he immediately reported this to the Deputy Chairman, who 
gave written Instructions on the note put-up by him for 
amme'ng for pasting on the door of the petitioner's house 


1, 190 7/mvr 12, 191S 


in the presence of two witnesses. Ex. M5 is filed showing 
his instructions. MW1 further deposes that as per the ins¬ 
tructions the same was pasted on the dour in the presence 
of tv,o witnesses and tl’/ir signatures were obtained in Ex. 
7,13. In his cross-examination also MW1 maintains that re¬ 
signation of the petitioner was accepted on 22-4-85 and they 
sen, intimation ot the same by registered post but the same 
was retui n.'d unserved. It is contended for the petitioner 
that the petitioner sent her resignation on 29-4-85 as per the 
Ex. Ml, but as per the evidence of MW1 her resignation 
was already accepted on 22-4-85. WWI gives evasive reply 
in her cross-examination about sending of this letter stating 
that after submitting her resignation she was under treatment 
in her sister's hoire and she was not residing at her address 
at the time when the acceptance of her resignation was inti¬ 
mated to her by post. She denies a suggestion that copy of 
the relieving orders and the acceptance letter of resignation 
for pasting on her door but this is contrary to the official 
record under Ex. M5 and the postal endorsement under Ex. 
M4. All lh ; s evidence clearly shows that she submitted 
letter withdrawing resignation on 29-4-85, by which time her 
resignation was already accepted on 22-4-85 itself and the 
same was intimated to her but she avoided to receive the said 
■ntimatlon. Further, a perusal of Ex, M3 dated 7-6-85 shows 
that she addressed a letter to the Chief Accounts Officer, 
Dock Labour Board for setting her P.F, and gratuity stating 
that her resignation was accented ThA letter is not denied 
by WVV] when confronted in her cross-examination but she 
says that it may be that she submitted this letter to the man¬ 
agement Tor settlenien of P.F, dues and graulty. Thus, it is 
evident Hint the petitioner sent this letter under Ex. M3 dated 
7 6-8S asking the management to settle her P.F, and gratuity 
as her resignation was accepted. All these circumstances show 
that the services of the re*it : oner were not terminated ille¬ 
gally but she herself voluntarily resigned the job and with¬ 
drew the same after it was accepted. The management can¬ 
not he forced to reinstate her after her rsoignatinn is accepted 
and they mtid'e alternative orrangement for fVlivm the vacancy 
through employment exchange, Accordingly, I hold on this 
point that the petitioner's services are not illegally terminated. 

(8) Point No. 2 : In view of my findings on point No. I 
above, J hold that fhe petitioner is not entitled to any relief 
in these proceedings. 

(9) In the result, award 1 is passed answering the reference 
as follows: 

“The action of the management of Dock J.abour Board, 
Visakhapatnam in terminating the services of Smt. D. 
Venkata RathnBm, Ex-Female Nursing Orderly is 
justified and rhe is not entitled to any relief.” 

Dictated to steno transcribed by her given under mv hand 
and seal of the court this the 29th day of November, 1996. 

Smt. G. JAISHRFF. Chairman & Presiding Officer

This dispute coming on for final hearing before me in the 
piescncc of Sri Ch. Ramesh, advocate for workman and the 
management in person, and the management faicld during 
eriquiiy to attend the proceedings, upon hearing the arguments 
of workman and perusing the record the court passed the 
following ;— 

AWARD 

1. In this car.c, the Government of India referred the 
dispute lo this tribunal for adjudication under Sec. 10(l)(d) 
of the I.D. Act in the following terms :—■ 

‘‘Whether the action of the management of Divisional 
Enguecr, Telegraphs coxial project in terminating 
the services of Shri M. V. Ramana, cx-mazdoor is 
justified 7 Tf not to what re'ief the workman con¬ 
cerned is entitled to 7” 

2. The workman fried claim statement stating that he joined 
as Mazdoor on 21-11-85 with the respondent and continuously 
worked till 1-4-G8 on which date his services were terminat¬ 
ed without issumg une month notice or wages in lieu of 
notice and without payment of compensation and while 
returning his junior 3 . It is alleged that the respondent 
appointed fresh workmen subsequent to the date of termina¬ 
tion of the petitioner. Thm. the termination is illegal, arbi- 
fary and conlray to the provisions of Sec. 25F, 25G and 25H 
of the I.D, Act, 1947. The petitioner was kept out of em¬ 
ployment and could not secure alternative employment Inspito 
of bis best efforts and he remained unemployed, Thus, he 
pi“lys for reinstatement with continuity of service, back wages 
and consequential benefits. 

3, Counter is filed by the management. It is admitted 
that the workman worked with the management from 
21-11-85 to 31-3-88 cont : nuou‘ly. It is statc-d that he was 
employed as ensu'd Mazdoor who are taken for n specific 
work for a stipulated period at a particular place and 
therefore the question of ‘termination of Iris service 
v.'.e.f. 1-4-88 does not . arise . It is denied that the iun’ora 
of the petitioner were retained. It is pleaded that consequ-nt 
to the ban orders in the GO dated 22-6-88. no mazdoor 




950 THE GAZETTE OI INDIA : FEBRUARY 1, 1997. /MAGDA 12 , 1918 


[Pari II—Sec. 3(ii;] 


was. taken to Muster rolls whose initial date of taking to 
Muster jeoils wus alter 31-4-65. it is aonuiied tuat the 
case or the petitioner was reieircd to tne compeient aurhoi.ii 
tor itguiaiisautm consequent to mo receipt or onjl oraer to 
examine rue cases or iviazuoors tor reguiausation, it is 
starva mat on a rcv.ew oi me case ot me petitioner, it was 
inioiined that ne is not engiole tor reguiarisation as ho 
has not summed iOr tnc eligiDility conditions stipulated by 
the deportment of Telecom. 

4. On behalf of the workman, he got himself examined 
as VvWr ana got marked ruts. W1 to Wo. nor me manage¬ 
ment, no cv.ucnce is produced eitner oral or documemary 
anu me management railed to appear during tne corns© ol 
enquiry and aid not even cross examine wvvi. Tnc work- 
mc.it uted written arguments, perused written arguments 
filed by the workman and the evidence on record. 

5. The points that arise lor consideration are . 

U) Whether the service of workman is terminated 

illegally ? 

(2) To what relief is the workman entitled 7 

6 . Point No. 1.—The workman depose® as WW1 that he 
worked with the management irom 21-11-85 ltd 31-3-88 
continuously as proved by his service book under Ex. W6 and 
he was removed with ettect from 1-4-88 without issuing 
one month notice or wages in heu thereof and without pay¬ 
ment of compensation and while retaining hn juniors. He 
issued legal notice dated 26-10-92 in tins regard to the 
management praying for reinstatement with continuity of 
service with consequential benottts. Office copy of the 
said notice is filed under Ex. W-E W2 is the certificate of 
posting and Ex. W3 is the acknowledgement. He further 
filed a representation under Ex. W4 dated 19-8-93 to the 
respondent lor considering his case for reguiarisation. Ex. 
W5 is the letter forwarding his application to higher authori¬ 
ties for consideration marking a copy to him. All this 
evidence establishes that the petitioner worked for more 
than one year continuously with the respondent but he is 
removed without following the procedure under Sec. 25F 
of the I.D. Act, without giving him opo month notice or 
paying him in lieu of notice and without payiug retrench¬ 
ment compensation. Thus, the pettiioner is retrenched 
illegally and termination Is not justified in the circumstances. 

I hold on this point accordingly. , 

7. As the petitioner is retrenched illegally, he would 
be entitled in the normal course for reinstatement with back 
wages. Regarding back WRges, the learned counsel for the 
petitioner relies upon the decisions reported in 1984 (49) 
FLR page 323 (Bombay High Court), 1984 (1) LLN page 
768 (Patna High Court), 1978 LLJ page 474 (Supreme 
Court and 1990-2 ELN page 368 (Madras High Court! But 
in the Supreme Court judgment reported in 1978 L.L.J. page 
476 it is observed that in the very nature of things there may 
not be straight Jacket formula in awarding back wages, AH 
relevant considerations will enter the verdict. In this case 
WW1 deposes that he could not get any work inspite of 
attempt and he remained unemployed after his removal, 
but the same is unbelievable in ns much as the workman is a 
■person eking his livelihood by doing mazdoor work on daily 
rate wage and it is highly propable that ail these years he 
must be working as suen for earning livelihood. Further, 
he was removed in April, 1988 and now it is more than 8 
yrs. since he is removed. He issued lawyer notice also in 
1992 only. Thus, there arc latches also pn the part of the 
workman. In ..all these circumstances, I do not think it 
just and proper to award back wages to the workman but 
In the circumstances the ends of justice will be met by 
awarding suitable compensation having regard to the length 
of service rendered by him with the respondent. Accord¬ 
ingly, I quantify, the compensation at Rs. 2000. Conse¬ 
quently. I hold on this point that the petitioner is entitled 
for reinstatement and compensation of Rs. 2000 but without 
continuity of service and without back wages. The peti¬ 
tioner also complains about his sending representation for 
reguiarisation but he is not informed anything about the 
aatne. Thus, he is entitled for being considered Mr reguiari¬ 
sation and If he is held to be not entitled for reguiarisation 
on any grounds the same may be intimated to the petitioner 


by the management. The petitioner is entitled to this relief 
also accordingly. 

8. in the result, award is passed answering the leferenee 
as follows : “The action of the management of Divisional 
Engineer, Telegraphs, Coxial Project in terminating the ser¬ 
vices of Sri i\l. V. Ramana, ex-mazdoor is not justified and 
the workman is entitled to be reinstated as M a/door and 
further entitled for compensation of Rs. 20Q0 (Rupees two 
thousand only) and also to be considered for reguiarisation 
a® per tules and intimation of the same. The workman is 
not entitled to any baqk wages and continuity of service. 
Time for reinstatement aud payment of compensation of 
Rs. 2000 (Rupees two thousand only) is two months fjvim 
the date of receipt of copy of this award by the management, 
failing which the compensation amount shall carry interest 
@ 12 per cent p.a. 

Dictated to steno transcribed by her given under my hand 
and seal of the court this the 28th day of November, 1996.

3. It was alleged that the woiluncn were discharged from 
their duties of clerk but have been paid tho wages of unskilled 
worker and termed as workers of group ’D’. They were nol 
to be placed in this category and were clerks to be placed in the 
category of group *C\ They were being paid w ges it ->a • 
with the unskilled labourers while they were to be paid wages 
equal to the regular clerks. Th c Hon'blc Supreme Court 
has held that the worker should be paid wates recording to the 
work performed by him on the principle of equal pay for equa| 
work. The Management has also admitted the fact that 
enhanced rate of payment should be given to the workers who 
were discharging tho duties of same skill and skilled nature. 
So many workers got the enhanced rates of wages but not these 
workmen which they were entitled to. They were not regul- 
rised in thc service for so many years on the direction* of the 
Hon’ble Supreme Court the department had formulated a 
Scheme known as Casual Labourers (Grant of temporary 
status in Regularisation) Scheme. On the basis of this scheme 
they were required to be regularised. There was no mention 
about tho regularisation of semi-skilled and skilled employees 
for their regularisation and the demand of these workmen 
which was based on clear injustice. I-Iecce this refc ltncc. 


4. The Management in reply besides taki ng s omc lege 1 obje- 
tions alleged that the inference sought to be drawn by thici 
cl-f imants were not admitted for thc reasons given in detai 
in thc written statement. They are being considered for their 
regularisation under the scheme ordered by the Hon’blc 
Supreme Court. The allegations made by the workmen in 
the statement of claim were misconceived, Extraneous to the 
subject m itter lire dycovered under the scheme. The mana¬ 
gement was a department of Government of India and con¬ 
trolled by tho Central Government for Essential Services of 
the Telecommunication, The management further a legal 
hat they arc daily rated workmen and were being regularisde 


under the scheme ns such in Group D . They were teirig 
pa id thc wages of thc Group D employees ouiofth.se id 
claims 4 namely Om Pariah, Puran Clinic, Ram Avtar, Kcm 
Mohan Roy have. 1 Ir :ady been regularised as regular Mazdoor 
in Group “O' . The other 6 dally rated Mazdoors would 
M 30 be regularised in terms of thc aforesaid scheme. They 
no not belong to group ‘C category of employees which is u 
clerical cadre and they have no legal right to bo regularised in 
that cadre beyond the scheme of the Supreme Court. They 
were not performing tho duties of clerks and if they were 
assisting the clerks they were doing so to shirk from handwork 
as done by daily ratal inazdoors which was their employment. 
Their duties were different from thc responsibility of clerical 
eedreAnd could not be absorbed in the clerical cadre. 

5. The workmen absented on 27.6.94 and nobody appeared 
on their behalf They were ordered to bo proceeded against 
exparte. Thereafter on 30.11.95 application was filed for sett¬ 
ing aside expane a nd none appeared on behalf of workmen o n

7. I have heard representative for tho management and ha\ e 

gone tlirough the record. 

8. A perusal of the evidence led by the management fully 
justifies thc stand of the management. The workmen have l c d 
absolutely no evidence and they were a dmitleuly not covered 
by the scheme formulated by the Hon’blc Supreme Court. 
The Management has stated on oath that four of them have 
already been regularised in the cadre they were working and 
the process for thc remaining six lor thc regularisation was 
also going on. In view of this situation there is absolutely 
no evidence on record to suggest that these workmen could Ire 
regularised in the clerical cadre. The action of the manage¬ 
ment was fully justified and there was no ground to interfere 
in that process- They were not entiled to any relief as the 
action of the management stand justified. Parties are, how¬ 
ever, left to bear their own costs.

Shri R. C. Gautam—■-idongwith Hie workman.

AWARD 

The Central Government in the Ministry of Labour vide 
its Order No. L-42012/142/92-LR. (DU), dated 7th Decem¬ 
ber, 1993 has referred the following Industrial dispute to this 
Tribunal for adjudication : 

"‘Whether the termination of services of Smt. Bimla Sahi 
by the Management of Archaeological Survey of 
India with effect from 1st January, 1992 Is justified? 


If not, to what relief Smt. Bimla Sahi is entitled 

to ?” 

2. The case of the workman as stated in the statement 
of claim was that she was appointed as a casual worker at 
Archaeological Survey of India, Purann Qilla, New Delhi 
on 1st January, 1991. She worked till 28th March, 1992 
and has put in more than 240 days of continuous service in 
the Department. Her services were terminated from 28th 
March, 1992 without any ground. No notice or notice pay 
was ever paid to her before termination. 

3. Notice was scnL to the management on 16th May, 1994. 
One Satbir Singh appeared on behalf of the Management. 
Thereafter on 13tb June, 1994 none appeared on benalf of 
the management and the management was ordered to be pro¬ 
ceeded against cx-parte, The Management filed application 
for setting aside cx-parte order on 17th January, 1995 which 
was dismissed. 

4. The workman then filed affidavit in support of her evi¬ 
dence and appeared as her own witness ns WW1. 

5. I have heard the representative for the workman and 
have gone through the record. 

6 . From the statement of the workman I am satisfied that 
she worked with the management for more than 240 days of 
continuous service. No notice was given to her before termi¬ 
nation nor any notice pay was paid to her. On the basis 
of her sworn testimony and in the absence of any evidence 
led by the management who was proceeded ex-parte I hold 
that the action of the management was not justified. She 
was entitled to be reinstated bat she has led no evidence 
that she remained unemployed throughout the period. I, 
therefore, hold that she be re-employed without any back 
wages. The management is also burdened with Rs. 500 as 
costs in this case, Award accordingly, 

GANPATT SHARMA, Presiding Officer 

January 6, 1997.
